\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{matrix}\qquad\quad\begin{matrix}&\mathrm{coleg}_{\lambda}(b)=r-1,\\
\\
\ \\
\mathrm{coarm}_{\lambda}(b)=c-1,&b=(r,c),&\mathrm{arm}_{\lambda}(b)=\lambda_{r}-c,\\
\\
\ \\
&\mathrm{leg}_{\lambda}(b)=\lambda_{c}^{\prime}-r.\end{matrix}

The
hook length

h(b)
and the
content

c(b)
of the box
b
are defined by

h(b)=\mathrm{arm_{\lambda}}(b)+\mathrm{leg}_{\lambda}(b)+1\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad c(b)=\mathrm{coarm}_{\lambda}(b)-\mathrm{coleg}_{\lambda}(b).

Theorem 5.3
.

Let
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}_{\geq 0}
with

\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{n}
. Then

\displaystyle P_{\lambda}(1,t,t^{2},\ldots,t^{n-1};q,t)

\displaystyle=t^{n(\lambda)}\prod_{b\in\lambda}\frac{1-q^{\mathrm{coarm}_{\lambda}(b)}t^{n-\mathrm{coleg}_{\lambda}(b)}}{1-q^{\mathrm{arm}_{\lambda}(b)}t^{\mathrm{leg}_{\lambda}(b)+1}}.

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{n}
and let
\lambda
be the weakly decreasing rearrangment of
\mu
.
Then

\displaystyle E_{\mu}(1,t,t^{2},\ldots,t^{n-1};q,t)

\displaystyle=t^{n(\lambda)}\prod_{(r,c)\in\mu}\frac{1-q^{c}t^{v_{\mu}(r)}}{1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-u_{\mu}(r,c)}}.

5.3
Proofs

5.3.1
Proof of the
c
-function formula

Theorem
5.1
.

Let
\mu,\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
with
\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{n}
.
Then

\displaystyle E_{\mu}(1,t,t^{2},\ldots,t^{n-1};q,t)

\displaystyle=t^{\frac{(n-1)}{2}|\lambda|}t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{0}(c^{Y^{-1}}_{u_{\mu}}),

\displaystyle P_{\lambda}(1,t,t^{2},\ldots,t^{n-1};q,t)

\displaystyle=t^{\frac{(n-1)}{2}|\lambda|}\mathrm{ev}^{t^{-1}}_{0}(c^{Y^{-1}}_{t_{\lambda}})\qquad\hbox{and}

\displaystyle A_{\lambda+\rho}(1,t,t^{2},\ldots,t^{n-1};q,t)

\displaystyle=0.

Proof.

For this proof use the realization of the polynomial representation
\mathbb{C}[X]
as an induced
module
\widetilde{H}\mathbf{1}_{Y}
via the
\widetilde{H}
-module isomorphism of (
4.3
). Then the creation formulas for
E_{\mu}
,
P_{\lambda}
and
A_{\lambda+\rho}

are

E_{\mu}=t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\tau^{\vee}_{u_{\mu}}\mathbf{1}_{0},\qquad P_{\lambda}=\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}}{W_{\lambda}(t)}\mathbf{1}_{0}E_{\lambda},\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad A_{\lambda+\rho}=t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}\varepsilon_{0}E_{\lambda+\rho}

(see Theorem
4.2
and (
4.4
)).

Let
\mathbf{1}_{X}
be a formal symbol which satisfies

\mathbf{1}_{X}T_{j}=t^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{1}_{X}
and
\mathbf{1}_{X}g^{\vee}=\mathbf{1}_{X}
,
for
j\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
.
Since
g^{\vee}=x_{1}T_{1}\cdots T_{n-1}
and

x_{i+1}=T_{i}x_{i}T_{i}
then

x_{1}=g^{\vee}T^{-1}_{n-1}\cdots T^{-1}_{1}
and

\mathbf{1}_{X}x_{i}=t^{-\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}t^{i-1}\mathbf{1}_{X},\qquad\hbox{for $i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}$.}

Thus, if
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
then

\mathbf{1}_{X}E_{\mu}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};q,t)=\mathbf{1}_{X}t^{-\frac{1}{2}(n-1)|\mu|}E_{\mu}(1,t,t^{2},\ldots,t^{n-1};q,t).

For
i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{X}\tau^{\vee}_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{X}\Big(T_{i}+\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}-t^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1-Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{i+1}}\Big)=\mathbf{1}_{X}\Big(t^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}-t^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1-Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{i+1}}\Big)=\mathbf{1}_{X}\Big(\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}-t^{\frac{1}{2}}Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{i+1}}{1-Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{i+1}}\Big)=\mathbf{1}_{X}c^{Y^{-1}}_{i,i+1}.

By (
4.10
),

c^{Y^{-1}}_{i,i+1}\mathbf{1}_{Y}=\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{0}(c^{Y^{-1}}_{i,i+1})\mathbf{1}_{Y}.

If
w\in W
and
\ell(s_{i}w)>\ell(w)
then

\mathbf{1}_{X}\tau^{\vee}_{i}\tau^{\vee}_{w}\mathbf{1}_{Y}=\mathbf{1}_{X}c^{Y^{-1}}_{i,i+1}\tau^{\vee}_{w}\mathbf{1}_{Y}=\mathbf{1}_{X}\tau^{\vee}_{w}c^{Y^{-1}}_{w^{-1}(i),w^{-1}(i+1)}\mathbf{1}_{Y}=\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{0}(c^{Y^{-1}}_{w^{-1}(i),w^{-1}(i+1)})\mathbf{1}_{X}\tau^{\vee}_{w}\mathbf{1}_{Y}.

By induction, conclude that
if
w\in W
and
w=s_{i_{1}}\cdots s_{i_{\ell}}
is
a reduced word for
w
then

\mathbf{1}_{X}\tau^{\vee}_{w}\mathbf{1}_{Y}=\mathbf{1}_{X}\tau^{\vee}_{i_{1}}\cdots\tau^{\vee}_{i_{\ell}}\mathbf{1}_{Y}=\mathbf{1}_{X}\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{0}(c^{Y^{-1}}_{w})\mathbf{1}_{Y}=\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{0}(c^{Y^{-1}}_{w})\mathbf{1}_{X}\mathbf{1}_{Y}.

Thus, by the creation formula
E_{\mu}=t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\tau^{\vee}_{u_{\mu}}\mathbf{1}_{Y}
,

\displaystyle t^{-\frac{1}{2}(n-1)|\mu|}E_{\mu}(1,t,\ldots,t^{n-1};q,t)\mathbf{1}_{X}\mathbf{1}_{Y}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{X}E_{\mu}\mathbf{1}_{Y}=\mathbf{1}_{X}t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\tau^{\vee}_{u_{\mu}}\mathbf{1}_{Y}=t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{0}(c^{Y^{-1}}_{u_{\mu}})\mathbf{1}_{X}\mathbf{1}_{Y}.

which completes the proof of the first statement.

Using the creation formula

P_{\lambda}=\dfrac{t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}}{W_{\lambda}(t)}\mathbf{1}_{0}E_{\lambda}
gives

\displaystyle t^{-\frac{1}{2}(n-1)|\lambda|}

\displaystyle P_{\lambda}(1,t,\ldots,t^{n-1};q,t)\mathbf{1}_{X}\mathbf{1}_{Y}=\mathbf{1}_{X}P_{\lambda}\mathbf{1}_{Y}=\mathbf{1}_{X}\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}}{W_{\lambda}(t)}\mathbf{1}_{0}E_{\lambda}\mathbf{1}_{Y}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{X}\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}W_{0}(t)}{W_{\lambda}(t)}E_{\lambda}\mathbf{1}_{Y}=t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\lambda})}\frac{W_{0}(t)}{W_{\lambda}(t)}\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{0}(c^{Y^{-1}}_{u_{\lambda}})\mathbf{1}_{X}\mathbf{1}_{Y}.

Since
v^{-1}_{\lambda}=(w_{0}w_{\lambda})^{-1}=w_{\lambda}w_{0}

and
t_{\lambda}=u_{\lambda}v_{\lambda}
then

\displaystyle\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}W_{0}(t)}{t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{\lambda})}W_{\lambda}(t)}\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{0}(c^{Y^{-1}}_{u_{\lambda}})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{ev}^{t^{-1}}_{0}\Big(\frac{c^{Y^{-1}}_{w_{0}})}{c^{Y^{-1}}_{w_{\lambda}}}\Big)\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{0}(c^{Y^{-1}}_{u_{\lambda}})=\mathrm{ev}^{t^{-1}}_{0}\Big(\frac{c^{Y^{-1}}_{w_{0}})}{c^{Y^{-1}}_{w_{\lambda}}}\Big)\mathrm{ev}^{t^{-1}}_{0}(v^{-1}_{\lambda}c^{Y^{-1}}_{u_{\lambda}})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{ev}^{t^{-1}}_{0}(c^{Y^{-1}}_{v_{\lambda}})\mathrm{ev}^{t^{-1}}_{0}(v^{-1}_{\lambda}c^{Y^{-1}}_{u_{\lambda}})=\mathrm{ev}^{t^{-1}}_{0}(c^{Y^{-1}}_{u_{\lambda}v_{\lambda}})=\mathrm{ev}^{t^{-1}}_{0}(c^{Y^{-1}}_{t_{\lambda}}).

The first equality comes from Proposition
4.6
which gives

t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{\lambda})}W_{\lambda}(t)=\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{0}(c^{Y}_{w_{\lambda}})=\mathrm{ev}^{t^{-1}}_{0}(c^{Y^{-1}}_{w_{\lambda}}),

and the second equality results from the fact that

if
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
i<j
then
t^{j-i}=(t^{-1})^{(n-j)-(n-i)}=(t^{-1})^{w_{0}(j)-w_{0}(i)}
,

which gives

\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{0}(c^{Y^{-1}}_{u_{\lambda}})=\mathrm{ev}^{t^{-1}}_{0}(v^{-1}_{\lambda}c^{Y^{-1}}_{u_{\lambda}}).

Using the creation formula
A_{\lambda+\rho}=t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}\varepsilon_{0}E_{\lambda+\rho}
gives

t^{-\frac{1}{2}(n-1)|\lambda|}A_{\lambda+\rho}(1,t,\ldots,t^{n-1};q,t)\mathbf{1}_{X}\mathbf{1}_{Y}=\mathbf{1}_{X}A_{\lambda+\rho}\mathbf{1}_{Y}=t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0}}\mathbf{1}_{X}\varepsilon_{0}E_{\lambda+\rho}\mathbf{1}_{Y}=0,

since
\mathbf{1}_{X}\varepsilon_{0}=0
.
Thus
A_{\lambda+\rho}(1,t,\ldots,t^{n-1};q,t)=0
.
∎

5.3.2
Proof of the root product formula

Corollary
5.2
.

Let
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}_{\geq 0}
. Denote by
\lambda
the decreasing rearrangement of
\mu
and
n(\lambda)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(i-1)\lambda_{i}
.
Then

P_{\lambda}(1,t,t^{2},\ldots,t^{n-1};q,t)=t^{n(\lambda)}\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\prod_{\ell=0}^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}-1}\frac{1-q^{\ell}t^{j-i+1}}{1-q^{\ell}t^{j-i}}

and

E_{\mu}(1,t,t^{2},\ldots,t^{n-1};q,t)=t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\prod_{(r,c)\in\mu}\prod_{i=1}^{u_{\mu}(r,c)}\frac{1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-i+1}}{1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-i}}.

Proof.

Let
\rho^{\vee}=\frac{1}{2}(n-1,n-3,\ldots,-(n-3),-(n-1))
. Then

\displaystyle n(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\langle(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n}),(0,1,\ldots,n-1)\rangle=\langle\lambda,-\rho^{\vee}+\hbox{$\frac{(n-1)}{2}$}(1,1,\ldots,1)\ \rangle=\hbox{$\frac{(n-1)}{2}$}|\lambda|-\langle\lambda,\rho^{\vee}\rangle

and, since
\ell(t_{\lambda})=\sum_{i<j}(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})
then

\displaystyle\ell(t_{\lambda})

\displaystyle=(n-1)\lambda_{1}+(n-2)\lambda_{2}+\cdots+(n-n)\lambda_{n}-\big((n-1)\lambda_{n}+(n-2)\lambda_{n-1}+\cdots+(n-n)\lambda_{1}\big)

\displaystyle=(n-1)\lambda_{1}+(n-3)\lambda_{2}+(n-5)\lambda_{3}+\cdots-(n-3)\lambda_{n-1}-(n-1)\lambda_{n}=\langle\lambda,2\rho^{\vee}\rangle,

so that

n(\lambda)=\hbox{$\frac{1}{2}$}(n-1)|\lambda|-\hbox{$\frac{1}{2}$}\ell(t_{\lambda}).

(5.3)

Since

\displaystyle\mathrm{ev}^{t^{-1}}_{0}

\displaystyle(c_{i,j+rn}^{Y^{-1}})=\mathrm{ev}^{t^{-1}}_{0}\Big(t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1-tq^{r}Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{j}}{1-q^{r}Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{j}}\Big)

\displaystyle=t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1-tq^{r}t^{-(i-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}t^{(j-1)-\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}}{1-q^{r}t^{-(i-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}t^{(j-1)-\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}}=t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1-q^{r}t^{j-i+1}}{1-q^{r}t^{j-i}}

then, using (
5.1
) and (
5.3
),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{(n-1)}{2}|\lambda|}\mathrm{ev}^{t^{-1}}_{0}(c_{t_{\lambda}}^{Y^{-1}})

\displaystyle=t^{\frac{(n-1)}{2}|\lambda|}t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(t_{\lambda})}\prod_{i<j}\prod_{r=0}^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}-1}\frac{1-q^{r}t^{j-i+1}}{1-q^{r}t^{j-i}}=t^{n(\lambda)}\prod_{i<j}\prod_{r=0}^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}-1}\frac{1-q^{r}t^{j-i+1}}{1-q^{r}t^{j-i}}.

The formula in the first statement follows then from Theorem
5.1
.

Again by Theorem
5.1
,

\displaystyle E_{\mu}(1,t,t^{2},\ldots,t^{n-1};q,t)

\displaystyle=t^{\frac{(n-1)}{2}|\mu|}t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\mathrm{ev}_{k\rho}(c_{u_{\mu}}^{Y^{-1}})=t^{\frac{(n-1)}{2}|\mu|-\frac{1}{2}\ell(u_{\mu})-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v_{\mu})}\Big(t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(u_{\mu})}\mathrm{ev}_{k\rho}(c_{u_{\mu}}^{Y^{-1}})\Big)

and, if
\lambda
is the weakly decreasing rearrangement of
\mu
then,
by (
5.3
),

\displaystyle\hbox{$\frac{(n-1)}{2}$}|\mu|-\hbox{$\frac{1}{2}$}\ell(u_{\mu})-\hbox{$\frac{1}{2}$}\ell(v_{\mu})

\displaystyle=\hbox{$\frac{(n-1)}{2}$}|\lambda|-\hbox{$\frac{1}{2}$}\ell(t_{\mu})=\hbox{$\frac{(n-1)}{2}$}|\lambda|-\hbox{$\frac{1}{2}$}\ell(t_{\lambda})=n(\lambda).

Then (
5.2
) gives that

t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(u_{\mu})}\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{0}(c_{u_{\mu}}^{Y^{-1}})
is equal to

\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{0}\Big(\prod_{(r,c)\in\mu}\prod_{j=1}^{u_{\mu}(r,c)}\frac{1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}tY^{-1}_{v_{\mu}(r)}Y_{j}}{1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}Y^{-1}_{v_{\mu}(r)}Y_{j}}\Big)=\prod_{(r,c)\in\mu}\prod_{j=1}^{u_{\mu}(r,c)}\frac{1-tq^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-j}}{1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-j}}.

(5.4)

∎

5.3.3
Proof of the bosonic hook formula

Theorem
5.3
.

Let
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}_{\geq 0}
with

\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{n}
. Then

\displaystyle P_{\lambda}(1,t,t^{2},\ldots,t^{n-1};q,t)

\displaystyle=t^{n(\lambda)}\prod_{b\in\lambda}\frac{1-q^{\mathrm{coarm}_{\lambda}(b)}t^{n-\mathrm{coleg}_{\lambda}(b)}}{1-q^{\mathrm{arm}_{\lambda}(b)}t^{\mathrm{leg}_{\lambda}(b)+1}}.

Proof.

In view of Corollary
5.2
the result will follow if we prove that

\prod_{i<j}\prod_{\ell=0}^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}-1}\frac{1-q^{\ell}t^{j-i+1}}{1-q^{\ell}t^{j-i}}=\prod_{b\in\lambda}\frac{1-q^{\mathrm{coarm}_{\lambda}(b)}t^{n-\mathrm{coleg}_{\lambda}(b)}}{1-q^{\mathrm{arm}_{\lambda}(b)}t^{\mathrm{leg}_{\lambda}(b)+1}}.

The left-hand side is

LHS=\prod_{i<j}\prod_{\ell=0}^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}-1}\frac{1-q^{\ell}t^{j-i+1}}{1-q^{\ell}t^{j-i}}=\prod_{r=1}^{n}\prod_{j=r+1}^{n}\prod_{\ell=0}^{\lambda_{r}-\lambda_{j}-1}\frac{1-tq^{\ell}t^{j-r}}{1-q^{\ell}t^{j-r}}.

Let
m
be the number of columns of length
n
in
\lambda
.
Let
r\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
. Switching the products over
j
and
\ell
gives

\prod_{j=r+1}^{n}\prod_{\ell=0}^{\lambda_{r}-\lambda_{j}-1}\frac{1-tq^{\ell}t^{j-r}}{1-q^{\ell}t^{j-r}}=\prod_{c=m+1}^{\lambda_{r}}\prod_{j=\lambda_{c}^{\prime}+1}^{n}\frac{1-tq^{\lambda_{r}-c}t^{j-r}}{1-q^{\lambda_{r}-c}t^{j-r}}=\prod_{c=m+1}^{\lambda_{r}}\frac{1-tq^{\lambda_{r}-c}t^{n-r}}{1-q^{\lambda_{r}-c}t^{\lambda^{\prime}_{c}+1-r}}

(5.5)

The definitions of arms, legs, coarms and colegs of boxes give that

\displaystyle RHS

\displaystyle=t^{n(\lambda)}\prod_{b=(r,c)\in\lambda}\frac{1-q^{\mathrm{coarm}_{\lambda}(b)}t^{n-\mathrm{coleg}_{\lambda}(b)}}{1-q^{\mathrm{arm}_{\lambda}(b)}t^{\mathrm{leg}_{\lambda}(b)+1}}=t^{n(\lambda)}\prod_{r=1}^{n}\prod_{c=1}^{\lambda_{r}}\frac{1-q^{c-1}t^{n-(r-1)}}{1-q^{\lambda_{r}-c}t^{\lambda_{c}^{\prime}-r+1}}

For
r\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
let
\ell=\lambda_{r}
and write

\displaystyle\prod_{c=1}^{\lambda_{r}}\frac{1-q^{c-1}t^{n-(r-1)}}{1-q^{\lambda_{r}-c}t^{\lambda_{c}^{\prime}-r+1}}=\frac{(1-q^{0}t^{n-(r-1)})(1-q^{1}t^{n-(r-1)})\cdots(1-q^{\lambda_{r}-1}t^{n-(r-1)})}{(1-q^{\lambda_{r}-1}t^{\lambda_{1}^{\prime}-r+1})\cdots(1-q^{1}t^{\lambda^{\prime}_{\ell-1}-r-1+1})(1-q^{0}t^{\lambda_{\ell}^{\prime}-r+1)}}

to observe that the last
m
factors in the numerator cancel with the first
m
terms in
the denominator. Thus

\displaystyle\prod_{c=1}^{\lambda_{r}}\frac{1-q^{c-1}t^{n-(r-1)}}{1-q^{\lambda_{r}-c}t^{\lambda_{c}^{\prime}-r+1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-q^{0}t^{n-(r-1)})(1-q^{1}t^{n-(r-1)})\cdots(1-q^{\lambda_{r}-m}t^{n-(r-1)})}{(1-q^{\lambda_{r}-m}t^{\lambda_{\ell-m}^{\prime}-r+1})\cdots(1-q^{1}t^{\lambda^{\prime}_{\ell-1}-r-1+1})(1-q^{0}t^{\lambda_{\ell}^{\prime}-r+1)}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{c=m+1}^{\lambda_{r}}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{r}-c}t^{n-(r-1)}}{1-q^{\lambda_{-}c}t^{\lambda_{c}^{\prime}-r+1}}=\prod_{c=m+1}^{\lambda_{r}}\frac{1-tq^{\lambda_{r}-c}t^{n-r}}{1-q^{\lambda_{-}c}t^{\lambda_{c}^{\prime}-r+1}}.

Since this is equal to the expression in (
5.5
), the result follows.
∎

5.3.4
Proof of the electronic hook formula

Theorem
5.4
.

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{n}
and let
\lambda
be the weakly decreasing rearrangment of
\mu
.
Then

\displaystyle E_{\mu}(1,t,t^{2},\ldots,t^{n-1};q,t)

\displaystyle=t^{n(\lambda)}\prod_{(r,c)\in\mu}\frac{1-q^{c}t^{v_{\mu}(r)}}{1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-u_{\mu}(r,c)}}.

Proof.

In view of Corollary
5.2
the result will follow if we prove that

\prod_{b=(r,c)\in\mu}\prod_{j=1}^{u_{\mu}(r,c)}\frac{1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-j+1}}{1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-j}}=\prod_{(r,c)\in\mu}\frac{1-q^{c}t^{v_{\mu}(r)}}{1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-u_{\mu}(r,c)}}.

For a single box
b=(r,c)
, the product

\displaystyle{\prod_{j=1}^{u_{\mu}(r,c)}\frac{1-tq^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-j}}{1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-j}}}

is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{(1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)})}{\cancel{(1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-1})}}

\displaystyle\frac{\cancel{(1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-1})}}{\cancel{(1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-2})}}\cdots\frac{\cancel{(1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-(u_{\mu}(r,c)-1)})}}{(1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-u_{\mu}(r,c)})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)}}{1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-u_{\mu}(r,c)}}.

For a single fixed row
r
, the product

\displaystyle{\prod_{c=1}^{\mu_{r}}(1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)})=\prod_{c=1}^{\mu_{r}}1-q^{c}t^{v_{\mu}(r)},}

and so

\displaystyle\prod_{(r,c)\in\mu}\prod_{j=1}^{u_{\mu}(r,c)}\frac{1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-j+1}}{1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-j}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{(r,c)\in\mu}\frac{1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)}}{1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-u_{\mu}(r,c)}}=\prod_{(r,c)\in\mu}\frac{1-q^{c}t^{v_{\mu}(r)}}{1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-u_{\mu}(r,c)}}.

∎

6
The inner product and the Weyl character formula

One of the amazing formulas of mathematics is the Weyl character formula which, in the type

GL_{n}
case says that the Schur polynomial is a quotient of two determinants (the denominator is
a Vandermonde determinant). Miraculously, the Weyl character formula generalizes to a similar quotient formula
for the bosonic Macdonald polynomial
P_{\lambda}(q,qt)
. In this section we prove this quotient formula
for
P_{\lambda}(q,qt)
,
following the same proof as in
[
Mac03
, §5]
but with some simplifications in notation.

6.1
The inner product
(,)_{q,t}

Define an involution
\overline{\phantom{T}}\colon\mathbb{C}[X]\to\mathbb{C}[X]
by

\displaystyle\overline{f}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};q,t)

\displaystyle=f(x_{1}^{-1},\ldots,x_{n}^{-1};q^{-1},t^{-1}),

Define

\Delta_{\infty}^{X}=\Delta^{X}_{\infty}(t)=\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\prod_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\frac{1-tq^{r}x_{i}x^{-1}_{j}}{1-q^{r}x_{i}x^{-1}_{j}},\qquad\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}=\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}(t)=\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\prod_{r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\frac{1-tq^{r}x^{-1}_{i}x_{j}}{1-q^{r}x^{-1}_{i}x_{j}},

\Delta_{0}^{X}=\Delta_{0}^{X}(t)=\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\frac{1-tx_{i}x^{-1}_{j}}{1-x_{i}x^{-1}_{j}}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\Delta_{0}^{X^{-1}}=\Delta_{0}^{X^{-1}}(t)=\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\frac{1-tx^{-1}_{i}x_{j}}{1-x^{-1}_{i}x_{j}}.

Define a scalar product
(\ ,\ )_{q,t}\colon\mathbb{C}[X]\times\mathbb{C}[X]\to\mathbb{C}(q,t)
by

(f_{1},f_{2})_{q,t}=\mathrm{ct}\Big(\frac{f_{1}\overline{f_{2}}}{\Delta_{\infty}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X}\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}}\Big),\qquad\hbox{where}\quad\mathrm{ct}(f)=(\hbox{constant term in $f$}),

(6.1)

for
f\in\mathbb{C}[X]
.
Proposition
6.1
shows that, in a suitable sense, the inner product

(,)_{q,t}
is nondegenerate and normalized Hermitian.

Proposition 6.1
.

(sesquilinear) If
f,g\in\mathbb{C}[X]
and
c\in\mathbb{C}[q^{\pm 1}]
then

(cf,g)_{q,t}=c(f,g)_{q,t},\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad(f,cg)_{q,t}=\overline{c}(f,g)_{q,t}.

(nonisotropy) If
f\in\mathbb{C}[X]
and
f\neq 0
then
(f,f)_{q,t}\neq 0
.

(nondegeneracy) If
F
is a subspace of
\mathbb{C}[X]
and
(,)_{F}\colon F\times F\to\mathbb{C}
is the
restriction of
(,)_{q,t}
to
F
, then
(,)_{F}
is nondegenerate.

(normalized Hermitian) If
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathbb{C}[X]
then

\frac{(f_{2},f_{1})_{q,t}}{(1,1)_{q,t}}=\overline{\Big(\frac{(f_{1},f_{2})_{q,t}}{(1,1)_{q,t}}\Big)}.

Proof.

(a)

Let
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathbb{C}[X]
and
c\in\mathbb{C}[q^{\pm 1},t^{\pm 1}]
. Then

(cf_{1},f_{2})_{q,t}=\mathrm{ct}(cf_{1}\overline{f_{2}})=c\cdot\mathrm{ct}(f_{1}\overline{f_{2}})=c(f,g)_{q,t}

and

(f_{1},cf_{2})_{q,t}=\mathrm{ct}(f_{1}\overline{cf_{2}})=\mathrm{ct}(f_{1}\bar{c}\overline{f_{2}})=\overline{c}\cdot\mathrm{ct}(f_{1}\overline{f_{2}})=\overline{c}(f,g)_{q,t}.

(b)

Let
f\in\mathbb{C}[X]
with
f\neq 0
. By clearing denominators appropriately, renormalize
f

so that
f
specializes to something nonzero at
q=1
. If

f=\sum_{\mu}f_{\mu}x^{\mu}\qquad\hbox{then}\qquad(f,f)_{1,1}=(f,f)_{1,1^{k}}=\sum_{\mu}|f_{\mu}|^{2}\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}.

Thus
(f,f)_{t}\neq 0
.

(c)

Let
f\in F
with
f\neq 0
. Since
(f,f)_{q,t}\neq 0
then there exists
p\in F
such that

(f,p)_{q,t}\neq 0
. Thus the restriciton of
(,)_{q,t}
to
F
is nondegenerate.

(d)

Let

\displaystyle{f_{1}=\sum_{\lambda}a_{\lambda}x^{\lambda}}
and
\displaystyle{f_{2}=\sum_{\mu}b_{\mu}x^{\mu}}

and

\frac{\Delta_{\infty}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X}\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}}{(1,1)_{q,t}}=\frac{\Delta_{\infty}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X}\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}}{\mathrm{ct}(\Delta_{\infty}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X}\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}})}=\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}d_{\mu}(q,t)x^{\mu}.

Then

\frac{(f_{2},f_{1})_{q,t}}{(1,1)_{q,t}}=\sum_{\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\overline{a_{\lambda}}b_{\mu}d_{\lambda-\mu}=\sum_{\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\overline{a_{\lambda}\overline{b_{\mu}}d_{\mu-\lambda}}=\overline{\Big(\frac{(f_{1},f_{2})_{q,t}}{(1,1)_{q,t}}\Big)}.

∎

6.2
The inner product characterization of
E_{\mu}
and
P_{\lambda}

Recall that the elements of
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
are partially ordered with the DBlex order given by

\lambda\leq\mu\quad\hbox{if }\qquad\begin{array}[]{c}\hbox{$\lambda^{+}<\mu^{+}$ in dominance order}\\
\hbox{or}\\
\hbox{$\lambda^{+}=\mu^{+}$ and $z_{\lambda}<z_{\mu}$ in Bruhat order.}\end{array}

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
.
The nonsymmetric Macdonald polynomial
E_{\mu}(q,t)
is the unique element of

\mathbb{C}[X]
such that

(a)

E_{\mu}(q,t)=x^{\mu}+(\hbox{lower terms in DBlex order})
;

(b)

If
\nu\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and
\nu<\mu
then
(E_{\mu}(q,t),x^{\nu})_{q,t}=0
.

Proof.

Let

V=\hbox{span}\{x^{\mu}\ |\ \hbox{$\nu\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}$ and $\nu\leq\mu$}\}
,

S=\hbox{span}\{x^{\nu}\ |\ \hbox{$\nu\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}$ and $\nu<\mu$}\}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad S^{\perp}=\{f\in\mathbb{C}[X]\ |\ \hbox{if $p\in S$ then $(f,p)_{q,t}=0$}\}.

Since the inner product
(,)_{q,t}
is nonisotropic then the restriction of
(,)_{q,t}
to
V

is nondegenerate and so
\mathrm{dim}(S^{\perp})=1
. Then the normalization of
E_{\mu}\in S^{\perp}

is determined by condition (a).
∎

For
\gamma\in(\mathbb{Z}^{n})^{+}
, define the
monomial symmetric polynomial

m_{\gamma}
by

m_{\gamma}=\sum_{\mu\in S_{n}\gamma}x^{\mu},\qquad\hbox{where the sum is over all distinct rearrangements of $\gamma$.}

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
\lambda\in(\mathbb{Z}^{n})^{+}
.
The symmetric Macdonald polynomial
P_{\lambda}
is the unique element of

\mathbb{C}[X]^{S_{n}}
such that

(a)

P_{\lambda}(q,t)=m_{\lambda}+(\hbox{lower terms in dominance order})
;

(b)

If
\gamma\in(\mathbb{Z}^{n})^{+}
and
\gamma<\lambda
then
(P_{\lambda}(q,t),m_{\gamma})_{q,t}=0
.

Proof.

The proof is completed in the same manner as the proof of Proposition
6.2
.
∎

6.3
Going up a level from
t
to
qt

As in (
4.6
) and (
3.20
), let

A_{\rho}=A_{\rho}(t)=\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}(x_{j}-tx_{i})\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad W_{0}(t)=\sum_{w\in S_{n}}t^{\ell(w)}.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
f,g\in\mathbb{C}[X]^{S_{n}}
so that
f
and
g
are symmetric polynomials. Then

(f,g)_{q,qt}=\frac{W_{0}(qt)}{W_{0}(t^{-1})}(A_{\rho}f,A_{\rho}g)_{q,t}.

Proof.

Letting

a_{\rho}(x)=\prod_{i<j}(x_{j}-x_{i})\quad\hbox{and}\quad a_{\rho}(x^{-1})=\prod_{i<j}(x_{i}-x_{j}),\qquad\hbox{then}\qquad\Delta_{\infty}^{X}(qt)=\frac{\Delta_{\infty}^{X}(t)}{\Delta_{0}^{X}(qt)a_{\rho}(x)}.

Then

\displaystyle(f,g)_{q,qt}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{ct}\Big(\frac{f\overline{g}}{\Delta^{X}_{\infty}(qt)\Delta^{X^{-1}}_{\infty}(qt)\Delta^{X}_{0}(qt)}\Big)=\mathrm{ct}\Big(f\overline{g}\frac{\Delta^{X}_{0}(qt)a_{\rho}(x)\Delta^{X^{-1}}_{0}(qt)a_{\rho}(x^{-1})}{\Delta^{X}_{\infty}(t)\Delta^{X^{-1}}_{\infty}(t)\Delta^{X}_{0}(qt)}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{ct}\Big(f\overline{g}\frac{a_{\rho}(x)a_{\rho}(x^{-1})}{\Delta^{X}_{\infty}(t)\Delta^{X^{-1}}_{\infty}(t)}\Delta^{X^{-1}}_{0}(qt)\Big)=\frac{W_{0}(qt)}{n!}\mathrm{ct}\Big(f\overline{g}\frac{a_{\rho}(x)a_{\rho}(x^{-1})}{\Delta^{X}_{\infty}(t)\Delta^{X^{-1}}_{\infty}(t)}\Big),

where the last equality follows from the fact that
if
H
is symmetric then

\displaystyle\mathrm{ct}(H\Delta^{X^{-1}}_{0}(t))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n!}\mathrm{ct}\Big(\sum_{w\in S_{n}}w(H\Delta^{X^{-1}}_{0}(t))\Big)=\frac{1}{n!}\mathrm{ct}\Big(H\sum_{w\in S_{n}}w(\Delta^{X^{-1}}_{0}(t))\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n!}\mathrm{ct}\Big(HW_{0}(t)\Big)=\frac{W_{0}(t)}{n!}\mathrm{ct}(H).

Similarly, using
A_{\rho}=\Delta^{X}_{0}(t)a_{\rho}(x)
gives

\displaystyle(A_{\rho}f,

\displaystyle A_{\rho}g)_{q,t}=\mathrm{ct}\Big(f\overline{g}\frac{\Delta^{X}_{0}(t)a_{\rho}(x)\Delta^{X^{-1}}_{0}(t)a_{\rho}(x^{-1})}{\Delta^{X}_{\infty}(t)\Delta^{X^{-1}}_{\infty}(t)\Delta^{X}_{0}(t)}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{ct}\Big(f\overline{g}\frac{a_{\rho}(x)a_{\rho}(x^{-1})}{\Delta^{X}_{\infty}(t)\Delta^{X^{-1}}_{\infty}(t)}\Delta^{X^{-1}}_{0}(t)\Big)=\frac{W_{0}(t)}{n!}\mathrm{ct}\Big(f\overline{g}\frac{a_{\rho}(x)a_{\rho}(x^{-1})}{\Delta^{X}_{\infty}(t)\Delta^{X^{-1}}_{\infty}(t)}\Big).

Comparing these expressions for

(f,g)_{q,qt}
and
(A_{\rho}f,A_{\rho}g)_{q,t}
gives the statement.
∎

6.4
Weyl character formula for Macdonald polynomials

Theorem 6.5
.

Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
with
\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{2}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{n}
. Then

P_{\lambda}(q,qt)=\frac{A_{\lambda+\rho}(q,t)}{A_{\rho}(t)}.

Proof.

Since
A_{\lambda+\rho}=t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}\varepsilon_{0}E_{\lambda+\rho}
then

A_{\lambda+\rho}\in\mathbb{C}[X]^{\mathrm{Fer}}
. Thus, by
Proposition
4.8
,

\hbox{there exists}\qquad f\in\mathbb{C}[X]^{S_{n}}\qquad\hbox{such that}\qquad A_{\lambda+\rho}=A_{\rho}f.

If
\mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
is such that the coefficient of
x^{\mu}
in
A_{\lambda+\rho}
is nonzero then

\mu\leq w_{0}(\lambda+\rho)
. Thus

f=m_{\lambda}+\hbox{(lower terms)}.

The
E
-expansion for
A_{\lambda+\rho}
in Proposition
4.7
gives that

A_{\lambda+\rho}=\sum_{\mu\in S_{n}(\lambda+\rho)}d_{\lambda+\rho}^{\mu}E_{\mu}=E_{w_{0}(\lambda+\rho)}+(\hbox{lower terms})

and, from the definitions of
A_{\rho}
and
m_{\nu}
,

A_{\rho}m_{\nu}=x^{w_{0}(\nu+\rho)}+(\hbox{lower terms}).

Since

(E_{w_{0}(\lambda+\rho)},x^{\gamma})_{q,t}=0
for
\gamma\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
with

\gamma<w_{0}(\lambda+\rho)
,
then

(A_{\rho}f,A_{\rho}m_{\nu})_{q,t}=(A_{\lambda+\rho},A_{\rho}m_{\nu})_{q,t}=0,\qquad\hbox{for $\nu\in(\mathbb{Z}^{n})^{+}$ with $\nu<\lambda$.}

Thus, by (
6.4
), since
f\in\mathbb{C}[X]^{S_{n}}
and
m_{\nu}\in\mathbb{C}[X]^{S_{n}}
then

(f,m_{\nu})_{q,qt}=\frac{W_{0}(qt)}{W_{0}(t^{-1})}(A_{\rho}f,A_{\rho}m_{\nu})_{q,t}=0,\qquad\hbox{for $\nu\in(\mathbb{Z}^{n})^{+}$ with $\nu<\lambda$.}

Thus, by Proposition
6.3
,
f=P_{\lambda}(q,qt)
.
∎

7
Norms and
c
-functions

The Macdonald polynomials form an incredible family of orthogonal polynomials
with respect to the inner product
(,)_{q,t}
defined in (
6.1
).
These orthogonal polynomials
generalize the Askey-Wilson polynomials to the multivariate case and to all affine root systems.
In this section we see how the
c
-functions enter in the formulas for the norms of the Macdonald
polynomials. The special case of the norm of the polynomial
1
is the celebrated Macdonald
constant term conjecture
[
Mac82
]
. The generalization
[
Mac87
]
of the Macdonald
constant term conjectures to conjectures for the norms of the
P_{\lambda}
was astounding.
After much work on special cases,
the methods and tools of Heckman, Opdam, Macdonald, and Cherednik yielded a proof of the norm
conjecture (about 1994, see the Notes and References at the end of
[
Mac03
, §5]
).
This proof is exposited beautifully in
[
Mac03
, §5.8 and §5.9]
. In this section
we provide a type
GL_{n}
specific exposition, highlighting the role of the
c
-functions,
and using them to simplify some steps. The general framework of our exposition follows the proof
found in
[
Mac03
, §5.8]
.

7.1
Adjoints and orthogonality

For a linear operator
M\colon\mathbb{C}[X]\to\mathbb{C}[X]
, the
adjoint of
M

is the linear operator
M^{*}\colon\mathbb{C}[X]\to\mathbb{C}[X]
determined by

(Mf_{1},f_{2})_{q,t}=(f_{1},M^{*}f_{2})_{q,t},\qquad\hbox{for $f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathbb{C}[X]$,}

where the inner product on
\mathbb{C}[X]
is as defined in (
6.1
). Also, simplify the notation by letting

J_{q,t}=\frac{1}{\Delta_{\infty}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X}\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}}=\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\frac{(x_{i}x^{-1}_{j};q)_{\infty}}{(tx_{i}x^{-1}_{j};q)_{\infty}}\cdot\frac{(qx^{-1}_{i}x_{j};q)_{\infty}}{(qtx^{-1}_{i}x_{j};q)_{\infty}},\quad\text{ and }\quad\Delta_{ij}=\frac{1-tx_{i}x^{-1}_{j}}{1-x_{i}x^{-1}_{j}}.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
k\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
. Then,
as operators on
\mathbb{C}[X]
,

x_{i}^{*}=x^{-1}_{i},\qquad s^{*}_{k}=\frac{\Delta_{k},k+1}{\Delta_{k+1,k}}s_{k},\qquad T_{\pi}^{*}=T_{\pi}^{-1},\qquad T^{*}_{k}=T_{k}^{-1},\qquad Y_{i}^{*}=Y_{i}^{-1}.

Proof.

\circ

Adjoint of multiplication by
x_{i}
:

(x_{i}f,g)_{q,t}=\mathrm{ct}(x_{i}f\overline{g}J_{q,t})=\mathrm{ct}(f\cdot\overline{x_{i}^{-1}g}\cdot J_{q,t})=(f,x_{i}^{-1}g)_{q,t}.

\circ

Adjoint of
s_{k}
:
For
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
with
i\neq j
, note that

\overline{\Delta_{ij}}=\frac{1-t^{-1}x_{i}^{-1}x_{j}}{1-x^{-1}_{i}x_{j}}=\frac{tx_{i}x^{-1}_{j}-1}{t(x_{i}x^{-1}_{j}-1)}=t^{-1}\Delta_{ij}.

Therefore

\displaystyle s_{k}J_{q,t}

\displaystyle=s_{k}\Big(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\infty}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X}\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}}\Big)=s_{k}\Big(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\infty}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X^{-1}}\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}}\cdot\Delta_{0}^{X^{-1}}\Big)=\frac{1}{\Delta_{\infty}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X^{-1}}\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}}\cdot s_{k}(\Delta_{0}^{X^{-1}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Delta_{\infty}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X^{-1}}\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}}\cdot\Delta_{0}^{X^{-1}}\frac{\Delta_{k,k+1}}{\Delta_{k+1,k}}=\Big(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\infty}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X}\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}}\Big)\frac{\Delta_{k,k+1}}{\Delta_{k+1,k}}=J_{q,t}\frac{\Delta_{k,k+1}}{\Delta_{k+1,k}}.

Then

\displaystyle(s_{k}f_{1},f_{2})_{q,t}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{ct}\big((s_{k}f_{1})\overline{f_{2}}J_{q,t}\big)=\mathrm{ct}\big(s_{k}(f_{1}(s_{k}(\overline{f_{2}}J_{q,t})))\big)=\mathrm{ct}\big(f_{1}(s_{k}(f_{2}J_{q,t}))\big)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{ct}\Big(f_{1}(s_{k}\overline{f_{2}})J_{q,t}\frac{\Delta_{k,k+1}}{\Delta_{k+1,k}}\Big)=\mathrm{ct}\Big(f_{1}\overline{\frac{\Delta_{k,k+1}}{\Delta_{k+1,k}}(s_{k}f_{2})}J_{q,t}\Big)=(f_{1},\frac{\Delta_{k,k+1}}{\Delta_{k+1,k}}(s_{k}f_{2}))_{q,t}.

\circ

Adjoint of
T_{\pi}
: Recall that the action of
T_{\pi}
is given by

T_{\pi}=s_{1}s_{2}\cdots s_{n-1}y_{n}
and so

T_{\pi}x_{n}=q^{-1}x_{1},\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad T_{\pi}x_{i}=x_{i+1}\ \hbox{for $i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}$.}

Thus, if
i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
then
T_{\pi}x_{i}x^{-1}_{n}=qx_{i+1}x^{-1}_{1}
and

T_{\pi}qx^{-1}_{i}x_{n}=qq^{-1}x^{-1}_{i+1}x_{1}
. Therefore

\displaystyle T_{\pi}J_{q,t}

\displaystyle=T_{\pi}\Big(\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n-1}\frac{(x_{i}x^{-1}_{j};q)_{\infty}}{(tx_{i}x^{-1}_{j};q)_{\infty}}\cdot\frac{(qx^{-1}_{i}x_{j};q)_{\infty}}{(qtx^{-1}_{i}x_{j};q)_{\infty}}\Big)\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\frac{(x_{i}x^{-1}_{n};q)_{\infty}}{(tx_{i}x^{-1}_{n};q)_{\infty}}\cdot\frac{(qx^{-1}_{i}x_{n};q)_{\infty}}{(qtx^{-1}_{i}x_{n};q)_{\infty}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big(\prod_{2\leq i<j\leq n}\frac{(x_{i}x^{-1}_{j};q)_{\infty}}{(tx_{i}x^{-1}_{j};q)_{\infty}}\cdot\frac{(qx^{-1}_{i}x_{j};q)_{\infty}}{(qtx^{-1}_{i}x_{j};q)_{\infty}}\Big)\Big(\prod_{i=2}^{n}\frac{(qx_{i}x^{-1}_{1};q)_{\infty}}{(qtx_{i}x^{-1}_{1};q)_{\infty}}\cdot\frac{(qq^{-1}x^{-1}_{i}x_{1};q)_{\infty}}{(qtq^{-1}x^{-1}_{i}x_{1};q)_{\infty}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big(\prod_{2\leq i<j\leq n}\frac{(x_{i}x^{-1}_{j};q)_{\infty}}{(tx_{i}x^{-1}_{j};q)_{\infty}}\cdot\frac{(qx^{-1}_{i}x_{j};q)_{\infty}}{(qtx^{-1}_{i}x_{j};q)_{\infty}}\Big)\Big(\prod_{i=2}^{n}\frac{(qx^{-1}_{1}x_{i};q)_{\infty}}{(qtx^{-1}_{1}x_{i};q)_{\infty}}\cdot\frac{(x_{1}x^{-1}_{i};q)_{\infty}}{(tx_{1}x^{-1}_{i};q)_{\infty}}\Big)=J_{q,t}.

Thus

\displaystyle(T_{\pi}f_{1},f_{2})_{q,t}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{ct}\big((T_{\pi}f_{1})\overline{f_{2}}J_{q,t}\big)=\mathrm{ct}\big(T_{\pi}(f_{1}T_{\pi^{-1}}(\overline{f_{2}}J_{q,t}))\big)=\mathrm{ct}\big(f_{1}T_{\pi^{-1}}(\overline{f_{2}}J_{q,t})\big)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{ct}\big(f_{1}\big(T_{\pi^{-1}}(\overline{f_{2}})J_{q,t}\big)\big)=\mathrm{ct}\big(f_{1}\cdot\overline{T_{\pi^{-1}}f_{2}}\cdot J_{q,t}\big)=(f_{1},T_{\pi^{-1}}f_{2})_{q,t}.

\circ

Adjoint of
T_{k}
:

\displaystyle(T_{s_{k}})^{*}

\displaystyle=\big(-t^{-\frac{1}{2}}+(1+s_{k})t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Delta_{k+1,k})\big)^{*}=-t^{\frac{1}{2}}+t^{\frac{1}{2}}\overline{\Delta_{k+1,k}}(1+s_{k}^{*})

\displaystyle=-t^{\frac{1}{2}}+t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Delta_{k+1,k}\Big(1+\frac{\Delta_{k,k+1}}{\Delta_{k+1,k}}s_{k}\Big)=-t^{\frac{1}{2}}+(1+s_{k})t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Delta_{k+1,k}=T^{-1}_{s_{k}}.

\circ

Adjoint of
Y_{j}
:

Y_{1}^{*}=(T_{\pi}T_{n-1}\cdots T_{1})^{*}=T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T^{-1}_{n-1}T_{\pi}^{-1}=(T_{\pi}T_{n-1}\cdots T_{1})^{-1}=Y^{-1}_{1},

and
if
j\in\{2,\ldots,n\}
then

Y_{j}^{*}=(T^{-1}_{j-1}Y_{j-1}T^{-1}_{j-1})^{*}=T_{j-1}Y^{-1}_{j-1}T_{j-1}=(T^{-1}_{j-1}Y_{j-1}T^{-1}_{j-1})^{-1}=Y^{-1}_{j}.

∎

Next, we look at the adjoint operator of the bosonic and fermionic symmetrizers. Since
T_{i}^{-1}\mathbf{1}^{*}_{0}=T_{i}^{*}\mathbf{1}_{0}^{*}=(\mathbf{1}_{0}T_{i})^{*}=(t^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{1}_{0})^{*}=t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{1}_{0}
and

\mathbf{1}_{0}^{*}=T_{w_{0}}^{-1}+(\hbox{lower terms})=T_{w_{0}}+(\hbox{lower terms})\qquad\hbox{then}\qquad\mathbf{1}_{0}^{*}=\mathbf{1}_{0}.

Similarly, since
T^{-1}_{i}\varepsilon_{0}^{*}=T_{i}^{*}\varepsilon_{0}^{*}=(\varepsilon_{0}T_{i})^{*}=(-t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon_{0})^{*}=-t^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon_{0}^{*}
and

\varepsilon_{0}^{*}=T_{w_{0}}^{-1}+(\hbox{lower terms})=T_{w_{0}}+(\hbox{lower terms})\qquad\hbox{then}\qquad\varepsilon_{0}^{*}=\varepsilon_{0}.

The relations
Y_{i}^{*}=Y^{-1}_{i}
in combination with the knowledge of the eigenvalues for
the action of the
Y_{i}
on the
E_{\mu}
give the following orthogonality relations
for Macdonald polynomials.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. If
\mu\neq\lambda
then

(E_{\lambda},E_{\mu})_{q,t}=0
.

Let
\lambda,\mu\in(\mathbb{Z}^{n})^{+}
. If
\mu\neq\lambda
then

(P_{\lambda},P_{\mu})_{q,t}=0
.

Let
\lambda,\mu\in(\mathbb{Z}^{n})^{+}
. If
\mu\neq\lambda
then

(A_{\lambda+\rho},A_{\mu+\rho})_{q,t}=0
.

Proof.

Let
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
. Then, by Theorem
1.1
,

\displaystyle q^{-\lambda_{i}}t^{-(v_{\lambda}(i)-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}

\displaystyle(E_{\lambda},E_{\mu})_{q,t}=(Y_{i}E_{\lambda},E_{\mu})_{q,t}=(E_{\lambda},Y^{-1}_{i}E_{\mu})_{q,t}=(E_{\lambda},q^{\mu_{i}}t^{(v_{\mu}(i)-1)\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}E_{\mu})_{q,t}

\displaystyle=\overline{q^{\mu_{i}}t^{(v_{\mu}(i)-1)-\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}}(E_{\lambda},E_{\mu})_{q,t}=q^{-\mu_{i}}t^{-(v_{\mu}(i)-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}(E_{\lambda},E_{\mu})_{q,t}.

If
(E_{\lambda},E_{\mu})_{q,t}\neq 0
then
q^{-\lambda_{i}}=q^{-\mu_{i}}
for
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
.
Thus
\lambda_{i}=\mu_{i}
for
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and so
\lambda=\mu
(and
v_{\lambda}=v_{\mu}
).

Parts (b) and (c) follow from (a) and the
E
-expansions in Proposition
4.7
.
∎

7.2
Reductions for norms

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
.
Then

\frac{(E_{\mu},E_{\mu})_{q,t}}{(1,1)_{q,t}}=\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{0}(c_{u_{\mu}}^{Y}c_{u_{\mu}}^{Y^{-1}}).

Let
\lambda\in\left(\mathbb{Z}^{n}\right)^{+}

and let
\rho=(n-1,n-2,\ldots,1,0)
.
Then

\frac{(P_{\lambda},P_{\lambda})_{q,t}}{(E_{\lambda},E_{\lambda})_{q,t}}=\frac{W_{0}(t)}{W_{\lambda}(t)}t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v_{\lambda})}\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{\lambda}(c_{v_{\lambda}}^{Y})\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\frac{(A_{\lambda+\rho},A_{\lambda+\rho})_{q,t}}{(P_{\lambda+\rho},P_{\lambda+\rho})_{q,t}}=\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{\lambda+\rho}\Big(\frac{c^{Y^{-1}}_{w_{0}}}{c_{w_{0}}^{Y}}\Big).

Let
\lambda\in\left(\mathbb{Z}^{n}\right)^{+}
.
Then

\frac{(P_{\lambda}(q,qt),P_{\lambda}(q,qt))_{q,qt}}{(P_{\lambda+\rho}(q,t),P_{\lambda+\rho}(q,t))_{q,t}}=\frac{W_{0}(qt)}{W_{0}(t)}\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{\lambda+\rho}\Big(t^{\ell(w_{0})}\frac{c_{w_{0}}^{Y^{-1}}}{c_{w_{0}}^{Y}}\Big).

Proof.

(a)

Recalling the intertwiners
\tau^{\vee}_{i}
from (
1.12
) (see also the
proof of Proposition
3.4
),

(\tau^{\vee}_{i})^{*}=\Big(T_{i}+\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}-t^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1-Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{i+1}}\Big)^{*}=T_{i}^{*}+\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{1-Y_{i}Y^{-1}_{i+1}}=T_{i}^{-1}+\frac{(t^{-\frac{1}{2}}-t^{\frac{1}{2}})Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{i+1}}{1-Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{i+1}}=\tau^{\vee}_{i},

and

(\tau^{\vee}_{i})^{2}=\frac{(1-tY_{i}Y^{-1}_{i+1})(1-tY^{-1}_{i}Y_{i+1})}{(1-Y_{i}Y^{-1}_{i+1})(1-Y^{-1}Y_{i+1})}=c_{i,i+1}^{Y}c_{i,i+1}^{Y^{-1}}.

Then, using the creation formula for
E_{\mu}
(Theorem
4.2
),

\displaystyle(E_{\mu},E_{\mu})_{q,t}

\displaystyle=(t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\tau^{\vee}_{u_{\mu}}\mathbf{1}_{Y},t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(v^{-1}_{\mu})}\tau^{\vee}_{u_{\mu}}\mathbf{1}_{Y})_{q,t}=(\tau^{\vee}_{u^{-1}_{\mu}}\tau^{\vee}_{u_{\mu}}\mathbf{1}_{Y},\mathbf{1}_{Y})_{q,t}

\displaystyle=(c_{u_{\mu}}^{Y}c_{u_{\mu}}^{Y^{-1}}\mathbf{1}_{Y},\mathbf{1}_{Y})_{q,t}=\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{0}(c_{u_{\mu}}^{Y}c_{u_{\mu}}^{Y^{-1}})\cdot(1,1)_{q,t}.

(b)

Note that

\mathbf{1}_{0}^{2}=t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}W_{0}(t)\mathbf{1}_{0}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\varepsilon_{0}^{2}=(-1)^{\ell(w_{0})}t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}W_{0}(t)\varepsilon_{0}.

By Proposition
4.7
,

\displaystyle P_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in S_{n}\lambda}b_{\lambda}^{\mu}E_{\mu},

with

\displaystyle b_{\lambda}^{\lambda}=t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(v_{\lambda})}\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{\lambda}(c_{v_{\lambda}}^{Y}),

\displaystyle A_{\lambda+\rho}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu\in S_{n}(\lambda+\rho)}d_{\lambda+\rho}^{\mu}E_{\mu},

with

\displaystyle d_{\lambda+\rho}^{\lambda+\rho}=(-t^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\ell(w_{0})}\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{\lambda+\rho}(c_{w_{0}}^{Y^{-1}}).

Since
W_{\lambda}(t^{-1})=t^{-\ell(w_{\lambda})}W_{\lambda}(t)
and

\ell(w_{0})-\ell(w_{\lambda})=\ell(v_{\lambda})
then using
P_{\lambda}=\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}}{W_{\lambda}(t)}\mathbf{1}_{0}E_{\lambda}
gives

\displaystyle(P_{\lambda},P_{\lambda})_{q,t}

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}}{W_{\lambda}(t)}\mathbf{1}_{0}E_{\lambda},\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}}{W_{\lambda}(t)}\mathbf{1}_{0}E_{\lambda}\Big)_{q,t}=\frac{1}{W_{\lambda}(t)W_{\lambda}(t^{-1})}(\mathbf{1}_{0}^{2}E_{\lambda},E_{\lambda})_{q,t}

\displaystyle=\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}W_{0}(t)}{W_{\lambda}(t)W_{\lambda}(t^{-1})}(\mathbf{1}_{0}E_{\lambda},E_{\lambda})_{q,t}=\frac{t^{-\ell(w_{0})}W_{0}(t)}{W_{\lambda}(t^{-1})}(P_{\lambda},E_{\lambda})_{q,t}

\displaystyle=\frac{t^{-\ell(w_{0})}W_{0}(t)}{t^{-\ell(w_{\lambda})}W_{\lambda}(t)}b_{\lambda}^{\lambda}(E_{\lambda},E_{\lambda})_{q,t}=\frac{t^{-\ell(v_{\lambda})}W_{0}(t)}{W_{\lambda}(t)}t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(v_{\lambda})}\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{\lambda}(c_{v_{\lambda}}^{Y})(E_{\lambda},E_{\lambda})_{q,t}.

Similarly, using
A_{\lambda+\rho}=t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}\varepsilon_{0}E_{\lambda+\rho}
gives

\displaystyle(A_{\lambda+\rho},

\displaystyle\ A_{\lambda+\rho})_{q,t}=\big(t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}\varepsilon_{0}E_{\lambda+\rho},\ t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}\varepsilon_{0}E_{\lambda+\rho}\big)_{q,t}=\big(\varepsilon^{2}_{0}E_{\lambda+\rho},\ E_{\lambda+\rho}\big)_{q,t}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell(w_{0})}t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}W_{0}(t)\big(\varepsilon_{0}E_{\lambda+\rho},\ E_{\lambda+\rho}\big)_{q,t}=(-1)^{\ell(w_{0})}t^{-\ell(w_{0})}W_{0}(t)\big(A_{\lambda+\rho},\ E_{\lambda+\rho}\big)_{q,t}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell(w_{0})}W_{0}(t^{-1})d_{\lambda+\rho}^{\lambda+\rho}\big(E_{\lambda+\rho},\ E_{\lambda+\rho}\big)_{q,t}=W_{0}(t^{-1})t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{\lambda+\rho}(c_{w_{0}}^{Y^{-1}})\big(E_{\lambda+\rho},\ E_{\lambda+\rho}\big)_{q,t}.

Using

(P_{\lambda+\rho},P_{\lambda+\rho})_{q,t}=W_{0}(t)t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{\lambda+\rho}(c_{w_{0}}^{Y})(E_{\lambda+\rho},E_{\lambda+\rho})_{q,t}
gives

\frac{(A_{\lambda+\rho},A_{\lambda+\rho})_{q,t}}{(P_{\lambda+\rho},P_{\lambda+\rho})_{q,t}}=\frac{W_{0}(t^{-1})t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{\lambda+\rho}(c^{Y^{-1}}_{w_{0}})}{W_{0}(t)t^{-\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{\lambda+\rho}(c_{w_{0}}^{Y})}=\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{\lambda+\rho}\Big(\frac{c^{Y^{-1}}_{w_{0}}}{c_{w_{0}}^{Y}}\Big).

(c)

Using Proposition
6.4
and the Weyl character formula
(Theorem
6.5
),

\displaystyle(P_{\lambda}(q,qt),P_{\lambda}(q,qt))_{q,qt}=\frac{W_{0}(qt)}{W_{0}(t^{-1})}(A_{\rho}(t)P_{\lambda}(q,qt),A_{\rho}(t)P_{\lambda}(q,qt))_{q,t}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{W_{0}(qt)}{t^{-\ell(w_{0})}W_{0}(t)}(A_{\lambda+\rho}(q,t),A_{\lambda+\rho}(q,t))_{q,t}=\frac{W_{0}(qt)}{W_{0}(t)}\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{\lambda+\rho}\Big(t^{\ell(w_{0})}\frac{c_{w_{0}}^{Y^{-1}}}{c_{w_{0}}^{Y}}\Big)(P_{\lambda+\rho}(q,t),P_{\lambda+\rho}(q,t))_{q,t},

where the last equality follows from the second formula in (b).

∎

Evaluating the
c
-functions appearing in Proposition
7.3
gives the following corollary.

Corollary 7.4
.

(E_{\mu},E_{\mu})_{q,t}=\Big(\prod_{(r,c)\in\mu}\prod_{i=1}^{u_{\mu}(r,c)}\frac{(1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-i+1})(1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-i-1})}{(1-q^{\mu_{r}-c+1}t^{v_{\mu}(r)-i})^{2}}\Big)\cdot(1,1)_{q,t},

(P_{\lambda},P_{\lambda})_{q,t}=\frac{W_{0}(t)}{W_{\lambda}(t)}\Big(\prod_{i<j}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}t^{j-i-1}}{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}t^{j-i}}\Big)\cdot(E_{\lambda},E_{\lambda})_{q,t}.

(A_{\lambda+\rho},A_{\lambda+\rho})_{q,t}=W_{0}(t^{-1})\Big(\prod_{i<j}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}+j-i}t^{j-i+1}}{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}+j-i}t^{j-i}}\Big)\cdot(E_{\lambda+\rho},E_{\lambda+\rho})_{q,t}.

\frac{(P_{\lambda}(q,qt),P_{\lambda}(q,qt))_{q,qt}}{(P_{\lambda+\rho}(q,t),P_{\lambda+\rho}(q,t))_{q,t}}=\frac{W_{0}(qt)}{W_{0}(t)}\Big(\prod_{i<j}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}+j-i}t^{j-i+1}}{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}+j-i}t^{j-i-1}}\Big).

Proof.

Using that

c^{Y}_{ij}c^{Y^{-1}}_{ij}=\Big(\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}-t^{\frac{1}{2}}Y_{i}Y^{-1}_{j}}{1-Y_{i}Y^{-1}_{j}}\Big)\Big(\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}-t^{\frac{1}{2}}Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{j}}{1-Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{j}}\Big)=\frac{(1-tY_{i}Y^{-1}_{j})(1-t^{-1}Y_{i}Y^{-1}_{j})}{(1-Y_{i}Y^{-1}_{j})^{2}},

the formula for
(E_{\mu},E_{\mu})_{q,t}
follows from Proposition
7.3
(a)
and the computation in (
5.4
).
The formula for
(P_{\lambda},P_{\lambda})_{q,t}
follows from Proposition
7.3
(b)
and the computation in (
4.11
).
The formula for
(P_{\lambda},P_{\lambda})_{q,t}
follows from Proposition
7.3
(b)
and the computation in (
4.12
).

Using

\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{\mu}(Y_{i})=q^{-\mu_{i}}t^{-(v_{\mu}(i)-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}
gives

\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{\mu}(Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{j})=q^{\mu_{i}}t^{(v_{\mu}(i)-1)-\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}q^{-\mu_{j}}t^{-(v_{\mu}(j)-1)+\frac{1}{2}(n-1)}=q^{\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}}t^{v_{\mu}(i)-v_{\mu}(j)},

for
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
with
i\neq j
.
Then

t\frac{c_{ij}^{Y^{-1}}}{c_{ij}^{Y}}=t\frac{\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}-t^{\frac{1}{2}}Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{j}}{1-Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{j}}}{\frac{t^{-\frac{1}{2}}-t^{\frac{1}{2}}Y_{i}Y^{-1}_{j}}{1-Y_{i}Y^{-1}_{j}}}=t\frac{\frac{1-tY^{-1}_{i}Y_{j}}{1-Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{j}}}{\frac{Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{j}-t}{Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{j}-1}}=\frac{1-tY^{-1}_{i}Y_{j}}{t^{-1}(t-Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{j})}=\frac{1-tY^{-1}_{i}Y_{j}}{1-t^{-1}Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{j}}

Using that
w_{\lambda+\rho}=w_{0}
and
w_{0}(i)=n-i+1
then

\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{\lambda+\rho}\Big(t^{\ell(w_{0})}\frac{c_{w_{0}}^{Y^{-1}}}{c_{w_{0}}^{Y}}\Big)=\mathrm{ev}^{t}_{\lambda+\rho}\Big(\prod_{i<j}\frac{1-tY^{-1}_{i}Y_{j}}{1-t^{-1}Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{j}}\Big)=\prod_{i<j}\frac{1-tq^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}+j-i}t^{j-i}}{1-t^{-1}q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}+j-i}t^{j-i}}.

(7.1)

In view of Proposition
7.3
(c), this proves the formula for

\dfrac{(P_{\lambda}(q,qt),P_{\lambda}(q,qt))_{q,qt}}{(P_{\lambda+\rho}(q,t),P_{\lambda+\rho}(q,t))_{q,t}}
.
∎

7.3
Formulas for norms and the constant term

Theorem 7.5
.

Let
\lambda\in\left(\mathbb{Z}^{n}\right)^{+}

and let
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. Then

(P_{\lambda}(q,q^{k}),P_{\lambda}(q,q^{k}))_{q,q^{k}}=W_{0}(q^{k})\prod_{r=1}^{k-1}\mathrm{ev}^{q^{r}}_{\lambda+(k-r)\rho}\Big(q^{r\cdot\ell(w_{0})}\frac{c^{Y^{-1}}_{w_{0}}(q^{r})}{c^{Y}_{w_{0}}(q^{r})}\Big).

Alternatively,

(P_{\lambda}(q,q^{k}),P_{\lambda}(q,q^{k}))_{q,q^{k}}=W_{0}(q^{k})\cdot\prod_{i<j}\prod_{r=1}^{k-1}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}+r}q^{k(j-i)}}{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}-r}q^{k(j-i)}}.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
k
. The base case is
k=0
, where

(P_{\lambda}(q,q^{0}),P_{\lambda}(q,q^{0}))_{q,q^{0}}=(m_{\lambda},m_{\lambda})_{q,1}=W_{\lambda}(1).

Using Proposition
7.3
(c), the first step of the induction is

\displaystyle(P_{\lambda}(q,q),

\displaystyle P_{\lambda}(q,q))_{q,q}=\frac{(P_{\lambda}(q,q),P_{\lambda}(q,q))_{q,q}}{(P_{\lambda+\rho}(q,q^{0}),P_{\lambda+\rho}(q,q^{0}))_{q,q^{0}}}(P_{\lambda+\rho}(q,q^{0}),P_{\lambda+\rho}(q,q^{0}))_{q,q^{0}}

\displaystyle=\frac{W_{0}(q)}{W_{0}(1)}\mathrm{ev}_{\lambda+\rho}^{1}\Big(1^{\ell(w_{0})}\frac{c^{Y^{-1}}_{w_{0}}(q)}{c^{Y}_{w_{0}}(q)}\Big)(m_{\lambda+\rho},m_{\lambda+\rho})_{q,1}=\frac{W_{0}(q)}{W_{0}(1)}\cdot 1\cdot W_{0}(1)=W_{0}(q),

and the general induction step is

\displaystyle(P_{\lambda}(q,q^{k}),

\displaystyle P_{\lambda}(q,q^{k}))_{q,q^{k}}=\frac{(P_{\lambda}(q,q^{k}),P_{\lambda}(q,q^{k}))_{q,q^{k}}}{(P_{\lambda+\rho}(q,q^{k-1}),P_{\lambda+\rho}(q,q^{k-1}))_{q,q^{k-1}}}(P_{\lambda+\rho}(q,q^{k-1}),P_{\lambda+\rho}(q,q^{k-1}))_{q,q^{k-1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{W_{0}(q^{k})}{W_{0}(q^{k-1})}\mathrm{ev}_{\lambda+\rho}^{q^{k-1}}\Big(q^{(k-1)\ell(w_{0})}\frac{c_{w_{0}}^{Y^{-1}}(q)}{c_{w_{0}}^{Y}(q)}\Big)W_{0}(q^{k-1})\prod_{r=1}^{k-2}\mathrm{ev}^{q^{r}}_{\lambda+(k-1-r)\rho}\Big(q^{r\cdot\ell(w_{0})}\frac{c^{Y^{-1}}_{w_{0}}}{c^{Y}_{w_{0}}}\Big)

\displaystyle=W_{0}(q^{k})\prod_{r=1}^{k-1}\mathrm{ev}^{q^{r}}_{\lambda+(k-r)\rho}\Big(q^{r\cdot\ell(w_{0})}\frac{c^{Y^{-1}}_{w_{0}}}{c^{Y}_{w_{0}}}\Big).

In a similar manner to the computation in (
7.1
),

\mathrm{ev}^{q^{r}}_{\lambda+(k-r)\rho}\Big(q^{r\cdot\ell(w_{0})}\frac{c_{w_{0}}^{Y}}{c_{w_{0}}^{Y^{-1}}}\Big)=\prod_{i<j}\frac{1-q^{r}q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}+(k-r)(j-i)}q^{r(j-i)}}{1-q^{-r}q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}+(k-r)(j-i)}q^{r(j-i)}}=\prod_{i<j}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}+r}q^{k(j-i)}}{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}-r}q^{k(j-i)}}.

∎

Specializing Proposition
7.5

at
\lambda=0
provides a proof of Macdonald’s constant term conjectures.
This proof follows the same framework as the proof exposited in
[
Mac03
, §5.8]
.

Proposition 7.6
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and let
t=q^{k}
. Then

(1,1)_{q,q^{k}}=\mathrm{ct}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta^{X}_{\infty}\Delta^{X}_{0}\Delta^{X^{-1}}_{\infty}}\right)=\prod_{i=2}^{n}\genfrac{[}{]}{0.0pt}{0}{ik}{k}

and

\frac{1}{W_{0}(q^{k})}(1,1)_{q,q^{k}}=\mathrm{ct}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta^{X}_{\infty}\Delta^{X}_{0}\Delta^{X^{-1}}_{0}\Delta^{X^{-1}}_{\infty}}\right)=\prod_{h=2}^{n-1}\genfrac{[}{]}{0.0pt}{0}{hk-1}{k-1}.

Proof.

The first equality is the definition of the inner product
(1,1)_{q,q^{k}}
. Then

\displaystyle\prod_{i<j}

\displaystyle\prod_{r=1}^{k-1}\frac{1-q^{r}q^{k(j-i)}}{1-q^{-r}q^{k(j-i)}}=\prod_{h=1}^{n-1}\prod_{i<j\atop j-i=h}\frac{(1-q^{kh+1})\cdots(1-q^{kh+k-1})}{(1-q^{kh-1})\cdots(1-q^{kh-(k-1)})}

\displaystyle=\prod_{h=1}^{n-1}\frac{\big((1-q^{kh+1})\cdots(1-q^{kh+(k-1)})\big)^{n-h}}{\big((1-q^{k(h-1)+1})\cdots(1-q^{k(h-1)+(k-1)})\big)^{n-h}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{h=1}^{n-1}\frac{\big((1-q^{kh+1})\cdots(1-q^{kh+(k-1)})\big)^{n-h}}{\big((1-q^{k(h-1)+1})\cdots(1-q^{k(h-1)+(k-1)})\big)^{n-(h-1)}}\cdot(1-q^{k(h-1)+1})\cdots(1-q^{k(h-1)+(k-1)})

\displaystyle=\frac{\big((1-q^{k(n-1)+1})\cdots(1-q^{k(n-1)+(k-1)})\big)^{n-(n-1)}}{\big((1-q)\cdots(1-q^{k-1})\big)^{n-(1-1)}}\Big(\prod_{h=1}^{n-1}(1-q^{k(h-1)+1})\cdots(1-q^{k(h-1)+(k-1)})\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{(1-q^{kn-(k-1)})\cdots(1-q^{kn-1)})}{(q;q)_{k-1}}\Big)\prod_{h=1}^{n-1}\frac{(1-q^{kh-(k-1)})\cdots(1-q^{kh-1})}{(q;q)_{k-1}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{h=2}^{n-1}\genfrac{[}{]}{0.0pt}{0}{hk-1}{k-1}.

Then, using
1=P_{0}(q,q^{k})
and Theorem
7.5
,

\displaystyle(1,1)_{q,q^{k}}

\displaystyle=W_{0}(q^{k})\prod_{i=2}^{n}\genfrac{[}{]}{0.0pt}{0}{ik-1}{k-1}=\Big(\prod_{i=2}^{n}\frac{1-q^{ik}}{1-q^{k}}\Big)\prod_{i=2}^{n}\genfrac{[}{]}{0.0pt}{0}{ik-1}{k-1}=\prod_{i=2}^{n}\genfrac{[}{]}{0.0pt}{0}{ik}{k}.

∎

Remark 7.7
.

Converting to general
q
and
t
.

Define

\Delta^{Y}_{\infty}(t)=\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\prod_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{1-tq^{r}Y_{i}Y^{-1}_{j}}{1-q^{r}Y_{i}Y^{-1}_{j}},\qquad\Delta^{Y^{-1}}_{\infty}(t^{-1})=\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\prod_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{1-t^{-1}q^{r}Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{j}}{1-q^{r}Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{j}},

\Delta_{0}^{Y}(t)=\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\frac{1-tY_{i}Y^{-1}_{j}}{1-Y_{i}Y^{-1}_{j}}.

Using the notation
(z;q)_{\infty}=(1-z)(1-qz)(1-q^{2}z)\cdots
, then

\Delta^{Y}_{\infty}(t)=\prod_{i<j}\frac{(qtY_{i}Y^{-1}_{j};)_{\infty}}{(qY_{i}Y^{-1}_{j})_{\infty}}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\Delta^{Y^{-1}}_{\infty}(t^{-1})=\prod_{i<j}\frac{(qt^{-1}Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{j};)_{\infty}}{(qY^{-1}_{i}Y_{j})_{\infty}}.

Define an evaluation homomorphism
\mathrm{ev}_{q^{\lambda}t^{\rho}}\colon\mathbb{C}[Y]\to\mathbb{C}
by

\mathrm{ev}_{q^{\lambda}t^{\rho}}(Y_{i})=q^{\lambda_{i}}t^{n-j},\qquad\hbox{so that}\qquad\mathrm{ev}_{q^{\lambda}t^{\rho}}(Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{j})=q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}t^{j-i}.

Let
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
with
i<j
and let
t=q^{k}
.
Using

(x;q)_{\infty}=(1-x)(qx;q)_{\infty},\qquad\qquad(x;q)_{k}=\frac{(x;q)_{\infty}}{(q^{k}x;q)_{\infty}},

and

(x;q^{-1})_{k}=(q^{-(k-1)}x;q)_{k}=(q^{-k}qx;q)_{k}=(t^{-1}qx;q),

then

\displaystyle\prod_{r=1}^{k-1}\frac{1-xq^{r}}{1-xq^{-r}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{r=0}^{k-1}\frac{1-xq^{r}}{1-xq^{-r}}=\frac{(x;q)_{k}}{(x;q^{-1})_{k}}=\frac{(x;q)_{k}}{q^{-k}qx;q)_{k}}=\frac{(x;q)_{\infty}}{(q^{k}x;q)_{\infty}}\frac{(q^{k}q^{-k}qx;q)_{\infty}}{(q^{-k}qx;q)_{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(x;q)_{\infty}}{(q^{k}x;q)_{\infty}}\frac{(qx;q)_{\infty}}{(q^{-k}qx;q)_{\infty}}=\frac{(qx;q)_{\infty}}{(q^{k}qx;q)_{\infty}}\frac{(1-x)}{(1-q^{k}x)}\frac{(qx;q)_{\infty}}{(q^{-k}qx;q)_{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(x;q)_{\infty}}{(q^{k}x;q)_{\infty}}\frac{(qx;q)_{\infty}}{(q^{-k}qx;q)_{\infty}}=\frac{(qx;q)_{\infty}}{(tqx;q)_{\infty}}\frac{(1-x)}{(1-tx)}\frac{(qx;q)_{\infty}}{(t^{-1}qx;q)_{\infty}}.

Then using
\mathrm{ev}_{q^{\lambda}t^{\rho}}(Y_{i}Y^{-1}_{j})=q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}t^{j-i}=\mathrm{ev}_{q^{-\lambda}t^{-\rho}}(Y^{-1}_{i}Y_{j})
,
gives

\displaystyle\prod_{i<j}\prod_{r=1}^{k-1}\frac{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}+r}t^{j-i}}{1-q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}-r}t^{j-i}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i<j}\frac{(qq^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}t^{j-i};q)_{\infty}}{(tqq^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}t^{j-i};q)_{\infty}}\frac{(1-q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}t^{j-i})}{(1-tq^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}t^{j-i})}\frac{(qq^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}t^{j-i};q)_{\infty}}{(t^{-1}qq^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}t^{j-i};q)_{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{ev}_{q^{\lambda}t^{\rho}}\Big(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\infty}^{Y}(t)}\Big)\mathrm{ev}_{q^{\lambda}t^{\rho}}\Big(\frac{1}{\Delta_{0}^{Y}(t)}\Big)\mathrm{ev}_{q^{-\lambda}t^{-\rho}}\Big(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\infty}^{Y^{-1}}(t^{-1})}\Big).

Thus the last statement of Proposition
7.5
can be written in the form

(P_{\lambda}(q,t),P_{\lambda}(q,t))_{q,t}=W_{0}(t)\cdot\mathrm{ev}_{q^{\lambda}t^{\rho}}\Big(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\infty}^{Y}(t)}\Big)\mathrm{ev}_{q^{\lambda}t^{\rho}}\Big(\frac{1}{\Delta_{0}^{Y}(t)}\Big)\mathrm{ev}_{q^{-\lambda}t^{-\rho}}\Big(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\infty}^{Y^{-1}}(t^{-1})}\Big),

a formula which, since it holds for all
t=q^{k}
with
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, holds for general
t
.
∎

7.4
The symmetric inner product

Define involutions

\displaystyle\overline{\phantom{T}}\colon\mathbb{C}[X]\to\mathbb{C}[X]

by

\displaystyle\bar{f}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};q,t)

\displaystyle=f(x_{1}^{-1},\ldots,x_{n}^{-1};q^{-1},t^{-1}),

\displaystyle{}^{\sigma}\colon\mathbb{C}[X]\to\mathbb{C}[X]

by

\displaystyle f^{\sigma}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};q,t)

\displaystyle=f(x_{1}^{-1},\ldots,x_{n}^{-1};q,t),

\displaystyle{}^{t}\colon\mathbb{C}[X]\to\mathbb{C}[X]

by

\displaystyle f^{t}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};q,t)

\displaystyle=f(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};q^{-1},t^{-1}).

Define two scalar products
(\ ,\ )\colon\mathbb{C}[X]\times\mathbb{C}[X]\to\mathbb{C}(q,t)
and

\langle\ ,\ \rangle\colon\mathbb{C}[X]\times\mathbb{C}[X]\to\mathbb{C}(q,t)
by

(f_{1},f_{2})_{q,t}=\mathrm{ct}\Big(\frac{f_{1}\overline{f_{2}}}{\Delta_{\infty}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X}\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}}\Big)\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\langle f_{1},f_{2}\rangle_{q,t}=\frac{1}{|W_{0}|}\mathrm{ct}\Big(\frac{f_{1}f_{2}^{\sigma}}{\Delta_{\infty}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X^{-1}}\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}}\Big).

The following result
provides a comparison of
(,)_{q,t}
and
\langle,\rangle_{q,t}

as inner products on symmetric polynomials.

Proposition 7.8
.

Let
f,g\in\mathbb{C}[X]^{S_{n}}
so that
f
and
g
are symmetric polynomials.
Then

\langle f,g\rangle_{q,t}=\frac{1}{W_{0}(t)}(f,g^{t})_{q,t}.

Proof.

Let
f,g\in\mathbb{C}[X]^{S_{n}}
.
Equation (
4.7
) says

W_{0}(t)=\sum_{w\in W_{0}}w(t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}c_{w_{0}}^{X^{-1}}),\qquad\hbox{and using}\qquad\Delta_{0}^{X^{-1}}=t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}c_{w_{0}}^{X^{-1}},

gives

\displaystyle\langle f,g\rangle_{q,t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|W_{0}|}\mathrm{ct}\Big(\frac{fg^{\sigma}}{\Delta_{\infty}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X^{-1}}\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}}\Big)=\frac{1}{W_{0}(t)|W_{0}|}\mathrm{ct}\Big(\frac{f\overline{g}^{t}}{\Delta_{\infty}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X^{-1}}\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}}W_{0}(t)\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{W_{0}(t)|W_{0}|}\mathrm{ct}\Big(\frac{f\overline{g}^{t}}{\Delta_{\infty}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X^{-1}}\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}}\big(\sum_{w\in W_{0}}w(t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}c_{w_{0}}^{X^{-1}})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{W_{0}(t)|W_{0}|}\mathrm{ct}\Big(\sum_{w\in W_{0}}w\big(\frac{f\overline{g}^{t}}{\Delta_{\infty}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X^{-1}}\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}}t^{\frac{1}{2}\ell(w_{0})}c_{w_{0}}^{X^{-1}}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{W_{0}(t)|W_{0}|}\mathrm{ct}\Big(\sum_{w\in W_{0}}w\big(\frac{f\overline{g}^{t}}{\Delta_{\infty}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X}\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}}\big)\Big)=\frac{1}{W_{0}(t)}\mathrm{ct}\Big(\frac{f\overline{g}^{t}}{\Delta_{\infty}^{X}\Delta_{0}^{X}\Delta_{\infty}^{X^{-1}}}\Big)=\frac{1}{W_{0}(t)}(f,g^{t})_{q,t}.

∎
